It’s embarrassing, really, because I used to keep all that straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or down… I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, especially when I can’t keep things straight like I used to. But sad, exactly? I don’t know. Some days I’m just tired of feeling muddled, and then I start thinking about how organized I used to be with the church bake sale and all the little labels—oh, I had a whole system for that. But no, I don’t know that I’ve noticed feeling especially sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. I suppose I get a little worked up when I can’t remember whether I put something on the calendar, but that feels more like being annoyed with myself. Like last week I had all these little notes by the phone, and then I moved them because I was dusting—well, I meant to dust, anyway—and then I couldn’t find the one I needed. But anxious, as in nervous all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties with remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s really the main thing, I think. Recent things just don’t stay put the way they used to. I’ll think an appointment is at one time, or that an exhibit is on a certain day, and then I check and it’s not right at all. Or I’ll be sure I already called a volunteer, and then I can’t remember if I actually did or just meant to. It’s little things, but they add up, and it makes my head feel foggy.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

My watercolors are still sitting out, and I used to love fussing with those, getting the blues just right. And my tomato plants too—I used to check them every morning. Now I look at them and just… don’t really care. Same with line dancing, and even watching the cardinals out back. They come to the feeder and I know I used to enjoy that, but lately it all just sits there, and I can’t seem to make myself feel interested.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know that I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean, I feel bad when I forget something or don’t keep up with things the way I used to, but then I start thinking about how my mother used to say you should write everything down, and I have all these little scraps of paper around the house. Some of them are from years ago, actually. But guilt, no… not especially, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts are looser around the waist lately. Food just doesn’t sound good most days… I’ll make toast, or maybe soup, and then only pick at it. I used to be more regular about meals—well, especially when my husband liked supper at five sharp—but lately I just don’t seem to want much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my head just feels… foggy, I suppose. I keep mixing up simple things, like appointment times, or dates for exhibits—there was one I was sure was on a Thursday, but then I looked again and, no, that wasn’t right. And I’ve been getting turned around about which volunteer I already called. It’s embarrassing, really, because I used to keep all that straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or down… I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, especially when I can’t keep things straight like I used to. But sad, exactly? I don’t know. Some days I’m just tired of feeling muddled, and then I start thinking about how organized I used to be with the church bake sale and all the little labels—oh, I had a whole system for that. But no, I don’t know that I’ve noticed feeling especially sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. I suppose I get a little worked up when I can’t remember whether I put something on the calendar, but that feels more like being annoyed with myself. But anxious, as in nervous all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties with remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s really the main thing, I think. I keep mixing up simple things, like appointment times, or dates for exhibits—there was one I was sure was on a Thursday, but then I looked again and, no, that wasn’t right. And I’ve been getting turned around about which volunteer I already called. It’s embarrassing, really, because I used to keep all that straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or down… I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, especially when I can’t keep things straight like I used to. But sad, exactly? I don’t know. Some days I’m just tired of feeling muddled, and then I start thinking about how organized I used to be with the church bake sale and all the little labels—oh, I had a whole system for that. But no, I don’t know that I’ve noticed feeling especially sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. I suppose I get a little worked up when I can’t remember whether I put something on the calendar, but that feels more like being annoyed with myself. Like last week I had all these little notes by the phone, and then I moved them because I was dusting—well, I meant to dust, anyway—and then I couldn’t find the one I needed. But anxious, as in nervous all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties with remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s really the main thing, I think. Recent things just don’t stay put the way they used to. I’ll think an appointment is at one time, or that an exhibit is on a certain day, and then I check and it’s not right at all. Or I’ll be sure I already called a volunteer, and then I can’t remember if I actually did or just meant to. It’s little things, but they add up, and it makes my head feel foggy.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed by these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose overwhelmed is a fair word. It’s like my head gets foggy, and then I start mixing up simple things that used to be nothing—appointment times, or what day an exhibit is, or which volunteer I already called. And then I’ll go looking for one note and find an old grocery list instead, which reminds me I forgot the tea bags last time… but yes, the fogginess, that’s really what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think I have. My watercolors are still sitting out, and I used to love fussing with those, getting the blues just right. And my tomato plants too—I used to check them every morning. Now I look at them and just… don’t really care. Same with line dancing, and even watching the cardinals out back. They come to the feeder and I know I used to enjoy that, but lately it all just sits there, and I can’t seem to make myself feel interested.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know that I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean, I feel bad when I forget something or don’t keep up with things the way I used to, but then I start thinking about how my mother used to say you should write everything down, and I have all these little scraps of paper around the house. Some of them are from years ago, actually. But guilt, no… not especially, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts are looser around the waist lately. Food just doesn’t sound good most days… I’ll make toast, or maybe soup, and then only pick at it. I used to be more regular about meals—well, especially when my husband liked supper at five sharp—but lately I just don’t seem to want much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way… “most of the day” is hard to say. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but then I’ll get distracted by something like the mail piling up or remembering I need to call my sister—though I keep meaning to and then don’t. Depressed… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Oh… I suppose it’s been about four months now. It sort of started after that water damage at the house, when everything had to be torn up and we got pushed into the remodel. I kept thinking it would be just a week or two, but you know how contractors are—my husband used to say nothing in a house ever takes the time they tell you. It hasn’t been anywhere near that long… though time does get muddled when the days are all the same. I’d say this low, slowed-down feeling has been with me maybe three months or so. Or I’ll be sure I already called a volunteer, and then I can’t remember if I actually did or just meant to. It’s little things, but they add up, and it makes my head feel foggy.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed by these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose overwhelmed is a fair word. It’s like my head gets foggy, and then I start mixing up simple things that used to be nothing—appointment times, or what day an exhibit is, or which volunteer I already called. And then I’ll go looking for one note and find an old grocery list instead, which reminds me I forgot the tea bags last time… but yes, the fogginess, that’s really what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think I have. My watercolors are still sitting out, and I used to love fussing with those, getting the blues just right. And my tomato plants too—I used to check them every morning. Now I look at them and just… don’t really care. Same with line dancing, and even watching the cardinals out back. They come to the feeder and I know I used to enjoy that, but lately it all just sits there, and I can’t seem to make myself feel interested.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know that I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean, I feel bad when I forget something or don’t keep up with things the way I used to, but then I start thinking about how my mother used to say you should write everything down, and I have all these little scraps of paper around the house. Some of them are from years ago, actually. But guilt, no… not especially, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts are looser around the waist lately. Food just doesn’t sound good most days… I’ll make toast, or maybe soup, and then only pick at it. I used to be more regular about meals—well, especially when my husband liked supper at five sharp—but lately I just don’t seem to want much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way… “most of the day” is hard to say. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but then I’ll get distracted by something like the mail piling up or remembering I need to call my sister—though I keep meaning to and then don’t. Depressed… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Oh… I suppose it’s been about four months now. It sort of started after that water damage at the house, when everything had to be torn up and we got pushed into the remodel. I kept thinking it would be just a week or two, but you know how contractors are—my husband used to say nothing in a house ever takes the time they tell you. So… yes, about four months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not two years. It hasn’t been anywhere near that long… though time does get muddled when the days are all the same. I’d say this low, slowed-down feeling has been with me maybe three months or so. I remember because it was around when I stopped keeping up with little things the way I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently down or depressed for more than two weeks at any point in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. More than two weeks, definitely. The low, slowed-down part has been hanging around for about three months now, I’d say. It’s funny, I kept thinking it would lift once I got back into some kind of routine, but routines go out the window so easily at my age. So yes… it’s been longer than just a couple weeks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

It’s changed, yes. I used to love my watercolors—I'd sit by the window and fuss with the little shadows on flowers for half the afternoon. And my tomato plants, too… I’d be out there checking them like they were children. Now they’re just sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care.

From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF32.0, F33.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my head just feels… foggy, I suppose. I keep mixing up simple things, like appointment times, or dates for exhibits—there was one I was sure was on a Thursday, but then I looked again and, no, that wasn’t right. And I’ve been getting turned around about which volunteer I already called. It’s embarrassing, really, because I used to keep all that straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or down… I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, especially when I can’t keep things straight like I used to. But sad, exactly? I don’t know. Some days I’m just tired of feeling muddled, and then I start thinking about how organized I used to be with the church bake sale and all the little labels—oh, I had a whole system for that. But no, I don’t know that I’ve noticed feeling especially sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. I suppose I get a little worked up when I can’t remember whether I put something on the calendar, but that feels more like being annoyed with myself. Like last week I had all these little notes by the phone, and then I moved them because I was dusting—well, I meant to dust, anyway—and then I couldn’t find the one I needed. But anxious, as in nervous all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties with remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s really the main thing, I think. Recent things just don’t stay put the way they used to. I’ll think an appointment is at one time, or that an exhibit is on a certain day, and then I check and it’s not right at all. Or I’ll be sure I already called a volunteer, and then I can’t remember if I actually did or just meant to. It’s little things, but they add up, and it makes my head feel foggy.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed by these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose overwhelmed is a fair word. It’s like my head gets foggy, and then I start mixing up simple things that used to be nothing—appointment times, or what day an exhibit is, or which volunteer I already called. And then I’ll go looking for one note and find an old grocery list instead, which reminds me I forgot the tea bags last time… but yes, the fogginess, that’s really what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think I have. My watercolors are still sitting out, and I used to love fussing with those, getting the blues just right. And my tomato plants too—I used to check them every morning. Now I look at them and just… don’t really care. Same with line dancing, and even watching the cardinals out back. They come to the feeder and I know I used to enjoy that, but lately it all just sits there, and I can’t seem to make myself feel interested.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know that I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean, I feel bad when I forget something or don’t keep up with things the way I used to, but then I start thinking about how my mother used to say you should write everything down, and I have all these little scraps of paper around the house. Some of them are from years ago, actually. But guilt, no… not especially, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts are looser around the waist lately. Food just doesn’t sound good most days… I’ll make toast, or maybe soup, and then only pick at it. I used to be more regular about meals—well, especially when my husband liked supper at five sharp—but lately I just don’t seem to want much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way… “most of the day” is hard to say. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but then I’ll get distracted by something like the mail piling up or remembering I need to call my sister—though I keep meaning to and then don’t. Depressed… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Oh… I suppose it’s been about four months now. It sort of started after that water damage at the house, when everything had to be torn up and we got pushed into the remodel. I kept thinking it would be just a week or two, but you know how contractors are—my husband used to say nothing in a house ever takes the time they tell you. So… yes, about four months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not two years. It hasn’t been anywhere near that long… though time does get muddled when the days are all the same. I’d say this low, slowed-down feeling has been with me maybe three months or so. I remember because it was around when I stopped keeping up with little things the way I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently down or depressed for more than two weeks at any point in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. More than two weeks, definitely. The low, slowed-down part has been hanging around for about three months now, I’d say. It’s funny, I kept thinking it would lift once I got back into some kind of routine, but routines go out the window so easily at my age. So yes… it’s been longer than just a couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yes. I used to love my watercolors—I'd sit by the window and fuss with the little shadows on flowers for half the afternoon. And my tomato plants, too… I’d be out there checking them like they were children. Now they’re just sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care.\n\nEven line dancing, which I used to enjoy—well, I haven’t felt like going. And the cardinals out back, I used to watch them every morning with my coffee. Funny little things, the males so bright, like Christmas ornaments… but lately I just look out and it doesn’t do much for me. It all feels sort of far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide recently?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, death crosses your mind more when you’re my age—friends pass, you read the obituaries, you start recognizing too many names. My neighbor used to say the newspaper got shorter except for that section, which isn’t funny but it sort of is.\n\nAs for suicide… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of my thoughts like that. It’s not something I’ve sat down and… planned out, if that’s what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my head just feels… foggy, I suppose. I keep mixing up simple things, like appointment times, or dates for exhibits—there was one I was sure was on a Thursday, but then I looked again and, no, that wasn’t right. And I’ve been getting turned around about which volunteer I already called. It’s embarrassing, really, because I used to keep all that straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or down… I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, especially when I can’t keep things straight like I used to. But sad, exactly? I don’t know. Some days I’m just tired of feeling muddled, and then I start thinking about how organized I used to be with the church bake sale and all the little labels—oh, I had a whole system for that. But no, I don’t know that I’ve noticed feeling especially sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. I suppose I get a little worked up when I can’t remember whether I put something on the calendar, but that feels more like being annoyed with myself. Like last week I had all these little notes by the phone, and then I moved them because I was dusting—well, I meant to dust, anyway—and then I couldn’t find the one I needed. But anxious, as in nervous all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties with remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s really the main thing, I think. Recent things just don’t stay put the way they used to. I’ll think an appointment is at one time, or that an exhibit is on a certain day, and then I check and it’s not right at all. Or I’ll be sure I already called a volunteer, and then I can’t remember if I actually did or just meant to. It’s little things, but they add up, and it makes my head feel foggy.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed by these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose overwhelmed is a fair word. It’s like my head gets foggy, and then I start mixing up simple things that used to be nothing—appointment times, or what day an exhibit is, or which volunteer I already called. And then I’ll go looking for one note and find an old grocery list instead, which reminds me I forgot the tea bags last time… but yes, the fogginess, that’s really what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think I have. My watercolors are still sitting out, and I used to love fussing with those, getting the blues just right. And my tomato plants too—I used to check them every morning. Now I look at them and just… don’t really care. Same with line dancing, and even watching the cardinals out back. They come to the feeder and I know I used to enjoy that, but lately it all just sits there, and I can’t seem to make myself feel interested.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know that I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean, I feel bad when I forget something or don’t keep up with things the way I used to, but then I start thinking about how my mother used to say you should write everything down, and I have all these little scraps of paper around the house. Some of them are from years ago, actually. But guilt, no… not especially, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts are looser around the waist lately. Food just doesn’t sound good most days… I’ll make toast, or maybe soup, and then only pick at it. I used to be more regular about meals—well, especially when my husband liked supper at five sharp—but lately I just don’t seem to want much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way… “most of the day” is hard to say. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but then I’ll get distracted by something like the mail piling up or remembering I need to call my sister—though I keep meaning to and then don’t. Depressed… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Oh… I suppose it’s been about four months now. It sort of started after that water damage at the house, when everything had to be torn up and we got pushed into the remodel. I kept thinking it would be just a week or two, but you know how contractors are—my husband used to say nothing in a house ever takes the time they tell you. So… yes, about four months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not two years. It hasn’t been anywhere near that long… though time does get muddled when the days are all the same. I’d say this low, slowed-down feeling has been with me maybe three months or so. I remember because it was around when I stopped keeping up with little things the way I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently down or depressed for more than two weeks at any point in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. More than two weeks, definitely. The low, slowed-down part has been hanging around for about three months now, I’d say. It’s funny, I kept thinking it would lift once I got back into some kind of routine, but routines go out the window so easily at my age. So yes… it’s been longer than just a couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yes. I used to love my watercolors—I'd sit by the window and fuss with the little shadows on flowers for half the afternoon. And my tomato plants, too… I’d be out there checking them like they were children. Now they’re just sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care.\n\nEven line dancing, which I used to enjoy—well, I haven’t felt like going. And the cardinals out back, I used to watch them every morning with my coffee. Funny little things, the males so bright, like Christmas ornaments… but lately I just look out and it doesn’t do much for me. It all feels sort of far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide recently?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, death crosses your mind more when you’re my age—friends pass, you read the obituaries, you start recognizing too many names. My neighbor used to say the newspaper got shorter except for that section, which isn’t funny but it sort of is.\n\nAs for suicide… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of my thoughts like that. It’s not something I’ve sat down and… planned out, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your appetite over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. Food just doesn’t sound good most days. I’ll make a piece of toast or heat up a little soup, and then I just sort of pick at it.\n\nI noticed my skirts are looser too, which at first I thought maybe the elastic was wearing out—you know how those old waistbands get—but no, it’s more than that. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything. It’s just… eating feels like more trouble than it’s worth lately.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I think it’s both, but more than just motivation. Like, the things are still there—my paints are in the little tin, the brushes all washed and lined up, and I keep thinking I ought to sit down and do a flower or two. But when I look at them, there’s no pull in me.

I remember because it was around when I stopped keeping up with little things the way I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently down or depressed for more than two weeks at any point in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Some of them are from years ago, actually. But guilt, no… not especially, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts are looser around the waist lately. Food just doesn’t sound good most days… I’ll make toast, or maybe soup, and then only pick at it. I used to be more regular about meals—well, especially when my husband liked supper at five sharp—but lately I just don’t seem to want much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way… “most of the day” is hard to say. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but then I’ll get distracted by something like the mail piling up or remembering I need to call my sister—though I keep meaning to and then don’t. Depressed… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Oh… I suppose it’s been about four months now. It sort of started after that water damage at the house, when everything had to be torn up and we got pushed into the remodel. I kept thinking it would be just a week or two, but you know how contractors are—my husband used to say nothing in a house ever takes the time they tell you. So… yes, about four months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not two years. It hasn’t been anywhere near that long… though time does get muddled when the days are all the same. I’d say this low, slowed-down feeling has been with me maybe three months or so. I remember because it was around when I stopped keeping up with little things the way I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently down or depressed for more than two weeks at any point in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. More than two weeks, definitely. The low, slowed-down part has been hanging around for about three months now, I’d say. It’s funny, I kept thinking it would lift once I got back into some kind of routine, but routines go out the window so easily at my age. So yes… it’s been longer than just a couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yes. I used to love my watercolors—I'd sit by the window and fuss with the little shadows on flowers for half the afternoon. And my tomato plants, too… I’d be out there checking them like they were children. Now they’re just sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care.\n\nEven line dancing, which I used to enjoy—well, I haven’t felt like going. And the cardinals out back, I used to watch them every morning with my coffee. Funny little things, the males so bright, like Christmas ornaments… but lately I just look out and it doesn’t do much for me. It all feels sort of far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide recently?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, death crosses your mind more when you’re my age—friends pass, you read the obituaries, you start recognizing too many names. My neighbor used to say the newspaper got shorter except for that section, which isn’t funny but it sort of is.\n\nAs for suicide… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of my thoughts like that. It’s not something I’ve sat down and… planned out, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your appetite over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. Food just doesn’t sound good most days. I’ll make a piece of toast or heat up a little soup, and then I just sort of pick at it.\n\nI noticed my skirts are looser too, which at first I thought maybe the elastic was wearing out—you know how those old waistbands get—but no, it’s more than that. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything. It’s just… eating feels like more trouble than it’s worth lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, or is it more that you're just not motivated to do them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but more than just motivation. Like, the things are still there—my paints are in the little tin, the brushes all washed and lined up, and I keep thinking I ought to sit down and do a flower or two. But when I look at them, there’s no pull in me.\n\nSame with the tomato plants. I used to fuss over them terribly—my husband used to tease me that I talked to them more than I talked to him—but now they’re just… sitting out there. And line dancing, the cardinals at the feeder, all of it. I can’t seem to make myself care the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this low, slowed-down feeling?\n\nPatient: Oh… I’d say about three months now. It’s hard to pin down exactly, because one week sort of runs into the next, and then you look up and it’s a whole season gone by. I’m not sure, Doctor. I mean, I’ve had sad spells before—everybody does, don’t they? Especially once you get to my age, there are little losses here and there, and winters can feel long. I remember one January where the days were so gray I barely wanted to change the calendar, but then again I was also fussing over curtains in the living room then, so maybe that wasn’t the same thing.

This one feels… hard to compare. I can’t quite say if I’ve had it like this before.

I keep mixing up simple things, like appointment times, or dates for exhibits—there was one I was sure was on a Thursday, but then I looked again and, no, that wasn’t right. It’s embarrassing, really, because I used to keep all that straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or down… I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, especially when I can’t keep things straight like I used to. But sad, exactly? I don’t know. Some days I’m just tired of feeling muddled, and then I start thinking about how organized I used to be with the church bake sale and all the little labels—oh, I had a whole system for that. But no, I don’t know that I’ve noticed feeling especially sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. I suppose I get a little worked up when I can’t remember whether I put something on the calendar, but that feels more like being annoyed with myself. Like last week I had all these little notes by the phone, and then I moved them because I was dusting—well, I meant to dust, anyway—and then I couldn’t find the one I needed. But anxious, as in nervous all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties with remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s really the main thing, I think. Recent things just don’t stay put the way they used to. I’ll think an appointment is at one time, or that an exhibit is on a certain day, and then I check and it’s not right at all. Or I’ll be sure I already called a volunteer, and then I can’t remember if I actually did or just meant to. It’s little things, but they add up, and it makes my head feel foggy.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed by these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose overwhelmed is a fair word. It’s like my head gets foggy, and then I start mixing up simple things that used to be nothing—appointment times, or what day an exhibit is, or which volunteer I already called. And then I’ll go looking for one note and find an old grocery list instead, which reminds me I forgot the tea bags last time… but yes, the fogginess, that’s really what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think I have. My watercolors are still sitting out, and I used to love fussing with those, getting the blues just right. And my tomato plants too—I used to check them every morning. Now I look at them and just… don’t really care. Same with line dancing, and even watching the cardinals out back. They come to the feeder and I know I used to enjoy that, but lately it all just sits there, and I can’t seem to make myself feel interested.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know that I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean, I feel bad when I forget something or don’t keep up with things the way I used to, but then I start thinking about how my mother used to say you should write everything down, and I have all these little scraps of paper around the house. Some of them are from years ago, actually. But guilt, no… not especially, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts are looser around the waist lately. Food just doesn’t sound good most days… I’ll make toast, or maybe soup, and then only pick at it. I used to be more regular about meals—well, especially when my husband liked supper at five sharp—but lately I just don’t seem to want much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way… “most of the day” is hard to say. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but then I’ll get distracted by something like the mail piling up or remembering I need to call my sister—though I keep meaning to and then don’t. Depressed… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Oh… I suppose it’s been about four months now. It sort of started after that water damage at the house, when everything had to be torn up and we got pushed into the remodel. I kept thinking it would be just a week or two, but you know how contractors are—my husband used to say nothing in a house ever takes the time they tell you. So… yes, about four months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not two years. It hasn’t been anywhere near that long… though time does get muddled when the days are all the same. I’d say this low, slowed-down feeling has been with me maybe three months or so. I remember because it was around when I stopped keeping up with little things the way I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently down or depressed for more than two weeks at any point in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. More than two weeks, definitely. The low, slowed-down part has been hanging around for about three months now, I’d say. It’s funny, I kept thinking it would lift once I got back into some kind of routine, but routines go out the window so easily at my age. So yes… it’s been longer than just a couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yes. I used to love my watercolors—I'd sit by the window and fuss with the little shadows on flowers for half the afternoon. And my tomato plants, too… I’d be out there checking them like they were children. Now they’re just sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care.\n\nEven line dancing, which I used to enjoy—well, I haven’t felt like going. And the cardinals out back, I used to watch them every morning with my coffee. Funny little things, the males so bright, like Christmas ornaments… but lately I just look out and it doesn’t do much for me. It all feels sort of far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide recently?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, death crosses your mind more when you’re my age—friends pass, you read the obituaries, you start recognizing too many names. My neighbor used to say the newspaper got shorter except for that section, which isn’t funny but it sort of is.\n\nAs for suicide… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of my thoughts like that. It’s not something I’ve sat down and… planned out, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your appetite over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. Food just doesn’t sound good most days. I’ll make a piece of toast or heat up a little soup, and then I just sort of pick at it.\n\nI noticed my skirts are looser too, which at first I thought maybe the elastic was wearing out—you know how those old waistbands get—but no, it’s more than that. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything. It’s just… eating feels like more trouble than it’s worth lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, or is it more that you're just not motivated to do them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but more than just motivation. Like, the things are still there—my paints are in the little tin, the brushes all washed and lined up, and I keep thinking I ought to sit down and do a flower or two. But when I look at them, there’s no pull in me.\n\nSame with the tomato plants. I used to fuss over them terribly—my husband used to tease me that I talked to them more than I talked to him—but now they’re just… sitting out there. And line dancing, the cardinals at the feeder, all of it. I can’t seem to make myself care the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this low, slowed-down feeling?\n\nPatient: Oh… I’d say about three months now. It’s hard to pin down exactly, because one week sort of runs into the next, and then you look up and it’s a whole season gone by. But yes, that low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around for roughly three months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of being down or depressed nearly every day since they started about four months ago?\n\nPatient: Nearly every day, yes… though I think it’s been more like three months, not four. It’s sort of settled in, like a damp coat you can’t quite take off. Some mornings I think maybe I’ll shake it, and then the day just goes along the same way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling down or depressed like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, Doctor. I mean, I’ve had sad spells before—everybody does, don’t they? Especially once you get to my age, there are little losses here and there, and winters can feel long. I remember one January where the days were so gray I barely wanted to change the calendar, but then again I was also fussing over curtains in the living room then, so maybe that wasn’t the same thing.\n\nThis one feels… hard to compare. I can’t quite say if I’ve had it like this before.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"candidates": ["F32.0", "F33.0"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

I can’t quite say if I’ve had it like this before.\n\n[Last Question]\nHas this low, slowed-down feeling you're experiencing been continuous since it started about three months ago?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 79 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressive Episode Duration\nmanifestation: \"The low, slowed-down part has been with me for about three months now.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… it’s been with me about three months now. I mean, not that every single minute feels exactly the same—some parts of the day I’m distracted by making tea or looking for where I put my glasses, you know how that goes—but that low, slowed-down feeling hasn’t really gone away. It’s just sort of been there in the background all this time.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my head just feels… foggy, I suppose. I keep mixing up simple things, like appointment times, or dates for exhibits—there was one I was sure was on a Thursday, but then I looked again and, no, that wasn’t right. And I’ve been getting turned around about which volunteer I already called. It’s embarrassing, really, because I used to keep all that straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or down… I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, especially when I can’t keep things straight like I used to. But sad, exactly? I don’t know. Some days I’m just tired of feeling muddled, and then I start thinking about how organized I used to be with the church bake sale and all the little labels—oh, I had a whole system for that. But no, I don’t know that I’ve noticed feeling especially sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. I suppose I get a little worked up when I can’t remember whether I put something on the calendar, but that feels more like being annoyed with myself. Like last week I had all these little notes by the phone, and then I moved them because I was dusting—well, I meant to dust, anyway—and then I couldn’t find the one I needed. But anxious, as in nervous all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties with remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s really the main thing, I think. Recent things just don’t stay put the way they used to. I’ll think an appointment is at one time, or that an exhibit is on a certain day, and then I check and it’s not right at all. Or I’ll be sure I already called a volunteer, and then I can’t remember if I actually did or just meant to. It’s little things, but they add up, and it makes my head feel foggy.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed by these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose overwhelmed is a fair word. It’s like my head gets foggy, and then I start mixing up simple things that used to be nothing—appointment times, or what day an exhibit is, or which volunteer I already called. And then I’ll go looking for one note and find an old grocery list instead, which reminds me I forgot the tea bags last time… but yes, the fogginess, that’s really what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think I have. My watercolors are still sitting out, and I used to love fussing with those, getting the blues just right. And my tomato plants too—I used to check them every morning. Now I look at them and just… don’t really care. Same with line dancing, and even watching the cardinals out back. They come to the feeder and I know I used to enjoy that, but lately it all just sits there, and I can’t seem to make myself feel interested.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know that I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean, I feel bad when I forget something or don’t keep up with things the way I used to, but then I start thinking about how my mother used to say you should write everything down, and I have all these little scraps of paper around the house. Some of them are from years ago, actually. But guilt, no… not especially, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts are looser around the waist lately. Food just doesn’t sound good most days… I’ll make toast, or maybe soup, and then only pick at it. I used to be more regular about meals—well, especially when my husband liked supper at five sharp—but lately I just don’t seem to want much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way… “most of the day” is hard to say. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but then I’ll get distracted by something like the mail piling up or remembering I need to call my sister—though I keep meaning to and then don’t. Depressed… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Oh… I suppose it’s been about four months now. It sort of started after that water damage at the house, when everything had to be torn up and we got pushed into the remodel. I kept thinking it would be just a week or two, but you know how contractors are—my husband used to say nothing in a house ever takes the time they tell you. So… yes, about four months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not two years. It hasn’t been anywhere near that long… though time does get muddled when the days are all the same. I’d say this low, slowed-down feeling has been with me maybe three months or so. I remember because it was around when I stopped keeping up with little things the way I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently down or depressed for more than two weeks at any point in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. More than two weeks, definitely. The low, slowed-down part has been hanging around for about three months now, I’d say. It’s funny, I kept thinking it would lift once I got back into some kind of routine, but routines go out the window so easily at my age. So yes… it’s been longer than just a couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yes. I used to love my watercolors—I'd sit by the window and fuss with the little shadows on flowers for half the afternoon. And my tomato plants, too… I’d be out there checking them like they were children. Now they’re just sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care.\n\nEven line dancing, which I used to enjoy—well, I haven’t felt like going. And the cardinals out back, I used to watch them every morning with my coffee. Funny little things, the males so bright, like Christmas ornaments… but lately I just look out and it doesn’t do much for me. It all feels sort of far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide recently?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, death crosses your mind more when you’re my age—friends pass, you read the obituaries, you start recognizing too many names. My neighbor used to say the newspaper got shorter except for that section, which isn’t funny but it sort of is.\n\nAs for suicide… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of my thoughts like that. It’s not something I’ve sat down and… planned out, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your appetite over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. Food just doesn’t sound good most days. I’ll make a piece of toast or heat up a little soup, and then I just sort of pick at it.\n\nI noticed my skirts are looser too, which at first I thought maybe the elastic was wearing out—you know how those old waistbands get—but no, it’s more than that. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything. It’s just… eating feels like more trouble than it’s worth lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, or is it more that you're just not motivated to do them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but more than just motivation. Like, the things are still there—my paints are in the little tin, the brushes all washed and lined up, and I keep thinking I ought to sit down and do a flower or two. But when I look at them, there’s no pull in me.\n\nSame with the tomato plants. I used to fuss over them terribly—my husband used to tease me that I talked to them more than I talked to him—but now they’re just… sitting out there. And line dancing, the cardinals at the feeder, all of it. I can’t seem to make myself care the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this low, slowed-down feeling?\n\nPatient: Oh… I’d say about three months now. It’s hard to pin down exactly, because one week sort of runs into the next, and then you look up and it’s a whole season gone by. But yes, that low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around for roughly three months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of being down or depressed nearly every day since they started about four months ago?\n\nPatient: Nearly every day, yes… though I think it’s been more like three months, not four. It’s sort of settled in, like a damp coat you can’t quite take off. Some mornings I think maybe I’ll shake it, and then the day just goes along the same way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling down or depressed like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, Doctor.
